But yeah, I guess there is something there.\n\nLike if we have a big station meal, or I know I’ve had more water and the scale is up even a pound, I get this tight panic feeling. I know that probably sounds kind of extreme for one pound.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it. I don’t love admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak, but if the scale is up even a pound after a big station meal or just drinking more water, I get this tight panic feeling—like I’ve blown it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking people are going to notice and see me as soft, or like I’m not in control. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.

Sometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my eating has gotten a little more strict than I meant it to. It started as just trying to eat cleaner, you know, be in better shape for the lieutenant track.\n\nBut lately it’s been more like… black coffee, maybe a protein shake, and then a small dinner. Even on shift days, when I know I probably burned way more than that. I don’t know if that sounds dramatic or anything, but it feels like it’s gotten harder to keep it reasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s been noticeable. I don’t want to overstate it, but my gear and station clothes are definitely hanging looser than they used to.\n\nAnd, um… during the fitness review they flagged my weight as being too low for my build. I know that should probably be a sign to ease up, but part of me still feels like I could trim down a little more. I know that probably sounds not great.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m terrified of it all the time or anything. But yeah, I guess there is something there.\n\nLike if we have a big station meal, or I know I’ve had more water and the scale is up even a pound, I get this tight panic feeling. Like I’ve messed everything up… like people are going to look at me and think I’m getting soft or I’m not in control. I know that probably sounds kind of extreme for one pound.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it. I don’t love admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak, but if the scale is up even a pound after a big station meal or just drinking more water, I get this tight panic feeling—like I’ve blown it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking people are going to notice and see me as soft, or like I’m not in control. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. It started as just trying to eat cleaner, you know, be in better shape for the lieutenant track.\n\nBut lately it’s been more like… black coffee, maybe a protein shake, and then a small dinner. Even on shift days, when I know I probably burned way more than that. I don’t know if that sounds dramatic or anything, but it feels like it’s gotten harder to keep it reasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s been noticeable. I don’t want to overstate it, but my gear and station clothes are definitely hanging looser than they used to.\n\nAnd, um… during the fitness review they flagged my weight as being too low for my build. I know that should probably be a sign to ease up, but part of me still feels like I could trim down a little more. I know that probably sounds not great.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m terrified of it all the time or anything. But yeah, I guess there is something there.\n\nLike if we have a big station meal, or I know I’ve had more water and the scale is up even a pound, I get this tight panic feeling. Like I’ve messed everything up… like people are going to look at me and think I’m getting soft or I’m not in control. I know that probably sounds kind of extreme for one pound.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it. I don’t love admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak, but if the scale is up even a pound after a big station meal or just drinking more water, I get this tight panic feeling—like I’ve blown it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking people are going to notice and see me as soft, or like I’m not in control. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 27 / firefighter / associates\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my eating has gotten a little more strict than I meant it to. It started as just trying to eat cleaner, you know, be in better shape for the lieutenant track.\n\nBut lately it’s been more like… black coffee, maybe a protein shake, and then a small dinner. Even on shift days, when I know I probably burned way more than that. I don’t know if that sounds dramatic or anything, but it feels like it’s gotten harder to keep it reasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s been noticeable. I don’t want to overstate it, but my gear and station clothes are definitely hanging looser than they used to.\n\nAnd, um… during the fitness review they flagged my weight as being too low for my build. I know that should probably be a sign to ease up, but part of me still feels like I could trim down a little more. I know that probably sounds not great.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m terrified of it all the time or anything. But yeah, I guess there is something there.\n\nLike if we have a big station meal, or I know I’ve had more water and the scale is up even a pound, I get this tight panic feeling. Like I’ve messed everything up… like people are going to look at me and think I’m getting soft or I’m not in control. I know that probably sounds kind of extreme for one pound.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it. I don’t love admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak, but if the scale is up even a pound after a big station meal or just drinking more water, I get this tight panic feeling—like I’ve blown it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking people are going to notice and see me as soft, or like I’m not in control. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I know I’ve probably already said it, but I don’t really have those times where I’m eating a large amount really fast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes what feels “large” to me might not actually be large, so I don’t want to answer it wrong. But no, not episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten more food than most people would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, compared to most people, probably not.\n\nI might feel like it was more than I should’ve had, but if I really think about what other people eat in, like, a normal sitting, I don’t think it would stand out as a lot. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think I’ve had that kind of episode. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have times where you eat and then try to compensate for it, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t vomit or use laxatives or anything like that after eating.\n\nI might feel guilty or want to sort of “make up for it” in my head, but not in those ways you’re asking about. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. I mean, I don’t think most people would see it as unusually large. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I do exercise, but “excessive” feels kind of hard for me to judge.\n\nPart of me wants to say no, because it feels like I’m just trying to stay in shape, especially with work and everything. But I guess I don’t totally know where the line is for what you’d consider too much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine has increased since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nIt might feel like I’m more aware of it now, but I don’t know if it’s actually increased since I started eating less. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer… I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a clear increase.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I know I’ve probably already said it, but I don’t really have those times where I’m eating a large amount really fast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes what feels “large” to me might not actually be large, so I don’t want to answer it wrong. But no, not episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten more food than most people would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, compared to most people, probably not.\n\nI might feel like it was more than I should’ve had, but if I really think about what other people eat in, like, a normal sitting, I don’t think it would stand out as a lot. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think I’ve had that kind of episode. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have times where you eat and then try to compensate for it, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t vomit or use laxatives or anything like that after eating.\n\nI might feel guilty or want to sort of “make up for it” in my head, but not in those ways you’re asking about. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. I mean, I don’t think most people would see it as unusually large. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I do exercise, but “excessive” feels kind of hard for me to judge.\n\nPart of me wants to say no, because it feels like I’m just trying to stay in shape, especially with work and everything. But I guess I don’t totally know where the line is for what you’d consider too much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine has increased since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nIt might feel like I’m more aware of it now, but I don’t know if it’s actually increased since I started eating less. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer… I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a clear increase.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you're eating so little?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it.\n\nI don’t like admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak or something. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I know I’ve probably already said it, but I don’t really have those times where I’m eating a large amount really fast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes what feels “large” to me might not actually be large, so I don’t want to answer it wrong. But no, not episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten more food than most people would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, compared to most people, probably not.\n\nI might feel like it was more than I should’ve had, but if I really think about what other people eat in, like, a normal sitting, I don’t think it would stand out as a lot. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think I’ve had that kind of episode. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have times where you eat and then try to compensate for it, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t vomit or use laxatives or anything like that after eating.\n\nI might feel guilty or want to sort of “make up for it” in my head, but not in those ways you’re asking about. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. I mean, I don’t think most people would see it as unusually large. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I do exercise, but “excessive” feels kind of hard for me to judge.\n\nPart of me wants to say no, because it feels like I’m just trying to stay in shape, especially with work and everything. But I guess I don’t totally know where the line is for what you’d consider too much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine has increased since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nIt might feel like I’m more aware of it now, but I don’t know if it’s actually increased since I started eating less. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer… I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a clear increase.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you're eating so little?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it.\n\nI don’t like admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak or something. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I know I’ve probably already said it, but I don’t really have those times where I’m eating a large amount really fast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes what feels “large” to me might not actually be large, so I don’t want to answer it wrong. But no, not episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten more food than most people would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, compared to most people, probably not.\n\nI might feel like it was more than I should’ve had, but if I really think about what other people eat in, like, a normal sitting, I don’t think it would stand out as a lot. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think I’ve had that kind of episode. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have times where you eat and then try to compensate for it, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t vomit or use laxatives or anything like that after eating.\n\nI might feel guilty or want to sort of “make up for it” in my head, but not in those ways you’re asking about. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. I mean, I don’t think most people would see it as unusually large. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I do exercise, but “excessive” feels kind of hard for me to judge.\n\nPart of me wants to say no, because it feels like I’m just trying to stay in shape, especially with work and everything. But I guess I don’t totally know where the line is for what you’d consider too much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine has increased since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nIt might feel like I’m more aware of it now, but I don’t know if it’s actually increased since I started eating less. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer… I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a clear increase.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you're eating so little?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it.\n\nI don’t like admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak or something. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really thought of it as a rule, exactly. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because my eating has gotten a little more strict than I meant it to. It started as just trying to eat cleaner, you know, be in better shape for the lieutenant track.\n\nBut lately it’s been more like… black coffee, maybe a protein shake, and then a small dinner. Even on shift days, when I know I probably burned way more than that. I don’t know if that sounds dramatic or anything, but it feels like it’s gotten harder to keep it reasonable.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess it’s been noticeable. I don’t want to overstate it, but my gear and station clothes are definitely hanging looser than they used to.\n\nAnd, um… during the fitness review they flagged my weight as being too low for my build. I know that should probably be a sign to ease up, but part of me still feels like I could trim down a little more. I know that probably sounds not great.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I wouldn’t say I’m terrified of it all the time or anything. But yeah, I guess there is something there.\n\nLike if we have a big station meal, or I know I’ve had more water and the scale is up even a pound, I get this tight panic feeling. Like I’ve messed everything up… like people are going to look at me and think I’m getting soft or I’m not in control. I know that probably sounds kind of extreme for one pound.\n\nDoctor: Is the fear of gaining weight the main reason you restrict your food intake?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it. I don’t love admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak, but if the scale is up even a pound after a big station meal or just drinking more water, I get this tight panic feeling—like I’ve blown it.\n\nAnd then I start thinking people are going to notice and see me as soft, or like I’m not in control. So… yeah, I guess that fear does push me to cut back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is a big part of how you see yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… yeah, probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nBefore work, if I look in the mirror, I’m checking whether my waist looks tight enough, or whether my face looks… sharp, I guess. And if it doesn’t, it can kind of throw me off. I don’t feel as confident walking in, like I’m not as command-ready or squared away as I should be.\n\nI know that might sound dramatic, but… yeah, it affects how I see myself.\n\nDoctor: Do you make sure to exercise even when you're not scheduled for a shift or a training session?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I “make sure” to, exactly. I mean, on off days I’ll usually still do something, but I kind of tell myself it’s just to stay ready for the job, you know?\n\nLike it doesn’t feel like a formal training session every time. Sometimes it’s just a run or some bodyweight stuff. I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I guess I do feel better if I’ve moved that day, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound more intense than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you have a specific weight or body shape in mind that you're trying to achieve\n\nPatient: Not like an exact number written down or anything… I mean, I guess I have an idea of how I’m supposed to look.\n\nBefore work I’ll check the mirror and it’s more like… does my waist look tight enough, does my face look sharp enough. If it doesn’t, it kind of gets in my head and I don’t feel as confident going in. Like I don’t feel as… ready or squared away, if that makes sense. I know that probably sounds a little much.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in the way you probably mean.\n\nLike, I might have a meal and afterward feel like I had more than I should’ve, but I wouldn’t say it’s some huge amount in a short time or anything. At least, I don’t think so. I guess “excessive” is hard for me to judge sometimes, but I don’t really have episodes where I’m just eating a ton all at once.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve really lost control, no. At least not like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nSometimes I’ll feel uncomfortable after eating, like I should’ve had less, but during it I’m usually aware of what I’m doing. I might regret it afterward, but I wouldn’t say I’m out of control. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean… I know I’ve probably already said it, but I don’t really have those times where I’m eating a large amount really fast or anything like that.\n\nSometimes what feels “large” to me might not actually be large, so I don’t want to answer it wrong. But no, not episodes like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten more food than most people would in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, compared to most people, probably not.\n\nI might feel like it was more than I should’ve had, but if I really think about what other people eat in, like, a normal sitting, I don’t think it would stand out as a lot. I’m not trying to dodge the question—I just don’t think I’ve had that kind of episode. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have times where you eat and then try to compensate for it, like by vomiting or using laxatives?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t vomit or use laxatives or anything like that after eating.\n\nI might feel guilty or want to sort of “make up for it” in my head, but not in those ways you’re asking about. I’m trying to answer honestly—I don’t think that’s something I regularly do.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food that would be considered a large amount by others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way that other people would look at it and say it was a really large amount.\n\nI can get uncomfortable with what I ate, like it feels like too much to me, but I’m pretty sure that’s more my own reaction than the actual amount. I mean, I don’t think most people would see it as unusually large. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine is excessive?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I do exercise, but “excessive” feels kind of hard for me to judge.\n\nPart of me wants to say no, because it feels like I’m just trying to stay in shape, especially with work and everything. But I guess I don’t totally know where the line is for what you’d consider too much. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your exercise routine has increased since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything.\n\nIt might feel like I’m more aware of it now, but I don’t know if it’s actually increased since I started eating less. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer… I guess I’d say I haven’t noticed a clear increase.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of gaining weight the main reason you're eating so little?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably a big part of it.\n\nI don’t like admitting that, because it sounds kind of vain or weak or something. But if the scale is up even a pound after, like, a bigger meal at the station or just drinking more water, I get this tight panicky feeling… like I’ve messed everything up. Like people are going to notice and think I’m getting soft or that I don’t have control.\n\nI know that might not sound totally rational, but… yeah, I think that fear is a main reason I keep things so limited.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you need to exercise every day to feel okay about what you've eaten?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I need to every day… that sounds a little strong.\n\nI mean, there are definitely days where moving around or working out makes me feel better about things, especially if I feel like I ate more than I meant to. But I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I guess I haven’t really thought of it as a rule, exactly. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're exercising more frequently than what's required for your job?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to separate it, honestly. My job is already pretty physical, so some of it just feels like part of staying ready for that.\n\nI wouldn’t want to say I’m doing a lot extra if that makes it sound worse than it is. There are times I do more on my own, but I don’t know that I’d call it “more frequently than required.” Maybe… maybe a little sometimes? I’m not sure if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've eaten an amount of food in a short time that you couldn't stop or control?\n\nPatient: Not really, no… I mean, I’ve had times where I ate more than I planned and felt bad about it after, but I don’t think it’s like I couldn’t stop myself.\n\nIf anything I’m usually pretty aware of what I’m eating. Maybe too aware. But no, I don’t think I’ve had that out-of-control thing you’re asking about. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you regularly have episodes where you feel like you've lost control while eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that happens regularly. I mean, I can feel uncomfortable if I ate more than I meant to, but it’s not like I black out or can’t stop or anything.\n\nI’m usually pretty in my head about it, honestly, so “lost control” doesn’t feel like the right way to describe it. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
